  The harness now holds seventy-seven green assertions across seven families, with verdicts two equal, eight property, one not-adopted and four divergent.
  A process lesson is recorded as a development entry, because I made the same mistake three rounds running: inserting a block before another block by using that block own opening lines as the anchor silently ate the opener, and only a syntax check caught it. Anchor on a marker comment, re-state the consumed lines when the anchor must be code, and check syntax immediately after every edit.
  Still open: the library and read-library families; the stage-level precondition; the rest of the meeting happy path, which needs a member-turn harness or the heavy driver; six check items; the drafting conflicts; the voting-method set; user confirmation for the clinical and animal demotion; the real-machine re-run; version and release.
- ***** 323 G1 CLOSES: all fifty-six v5r tools are now named by an assertion - coverage computed, not claimed.
  The proof is a computation: list the registered tools, diff against the names the A/B file mentions, and require the difference to be empty. It went from fifty to fifty-six this round, and the script refuses to pass if anything is left over.
  The last six turned out to be among the most interesting: temporary authorisation on the v5r side names ONE enumerated command, an event SCOPE and a required reason, and revoking it writes an event that is broadcast rather than done silently. Our grant face has exactly that shape - grant, check, revoke, list, scoped to an event - and the roster face covers hire, dismiss, wake and role assignment, which is where the permanent changes live. Supervision names both a reason and a next step on one side and maps to the wake operation on the other, and a recorded resolution maps to the minutes and records surfaces.
  The verdict tally is now three equal, nine property, one not-adopted and four divergent, over eighty-six green assertions. The single remaining not-adopted is the meeting happy path walked step by step, and the reason it is not adopted is measured rather than assumed: the preset assigns its acting member only when its own scheduler starts a member turn, so that flow needs a member-turn harness or the heavy driver. It is named as unfinished work rather than hidden.
  A process note belongs with this entry: the script that wrote this record threw at its last line - a reference to a block-scoped variable from outside the block - AFTER the volume and the gate had already been handled and committed. The ledger entry was therefore missing from that commit and is written by a second, smaller one. The lesson is the same shape as the insertion lesson from the previous round: a script that fails late can leave a partially applied change, so it should be re-run after the fix and its effects verified one by one rather than assumed.
  Still open: the member-turn harness (or heavy driver) for the happy path; the stage-level precondition; six check items; the drafting conflicts; the voting-method set; user confirmation for the clinical and animal demotion; the real-machine re-run; version and release.
- ***** 324 THE HAPPY PATH RUNS, AND NOT_ADOPTED REACHES ZERO. The blocker was never that the flow was unreachable.
  Read from the source rather than guessed: the acting member is resolved from agent.id, looked up against each member childId; failing that, a session root IS the office; only an agent with no id at all falls back to the last-woken member. Every earlier attempt passed the session root, so every member-only tool answered office-not-found. Nothing about the mechanism was unreachable - the caller was simply not seated the way the preset seats one.
  The fixture fakes nothing: it lets the preset own scheduler start member turns by returning a unique childId per call, reads the member-to-childId map out of the roster, and then acts as that child. The state machine, the roster and the ballot are all the real ones.
  Measured end to end, each step as its own assertion: the institute starts, the roster publishes each childId, a seated member convenes a meeting, a seated member opens a poll with a floor of three, closing that poll with no votes reports min_votes_reached=false and fabricates no outcome, a second seated member votes and the receipt names who has not voted yet, and closing once the floor is met reports reached=true with a real tally.
  That also settles C3 by measurement instead of by reading: a vote that misses its floor is reported as not reaching it on both sides rather than becoming an outcome - v5r sets the flag false, and vmu keeps a three-valued tally whose undecided branch must carry a reason.
  The tally is now four equal, ten property and four divergent over ninety-four green assertions, with NO not-adopted item left. The last one did not need a heavy CLI driver after all; it needed the acting member to be seated.
  Still open: the stage-level precondition; six check items; the drafting conflicts; the voting-method set; user confirmation for the clinical and animal demotion; the real-machine re-run; version and release.
- ***** 325 DEEPER WITH THE SEATED FIXTURE: motions driven, D7 measured, and the verification order found to be proof-first.
  D7 stops being a checked-against item: in a light meeting a resolution motion is refused, the refusal text cites D7 and the gap number, and it names both ways out - make the meeting formal, or open a decision-type meeting. That is exactly the type-times-decision-gate shape the first slice predicted, and it is now code-level evidence rather than inference.
  A formal meeting turns out to be PARKED rather than convened: the call succeeds and reports that the meeting is held pending until its verification prerequisite clears. That is the very behaviour the first slice described from the outside, now measured from the inside.
  The verification order is proof-first, and this is the strongest structural finding of the round: proposing an object for verification is refused unless that object has already been formally proved or disproved AND locked by its own owner. So the chain is: record the proposition, have it locked, register the formal result, and only then debate and aggregate. The rule that debate does not decide truth is therefore structural on that side rather than merely stated - the debate cannot begin until the machine result exists.
  Motions were driven too: a motion records how many seconds it needs and who has seconded it, and once that count is met it CARRIES.
  The tally is now five equal, eleven property and four divergent over a hundred and one green assertions.
  Still open: driving the record-and-lock half of the verification chain so the debate itself can be compared end to end; the stage-level precondition; six check items; the drafting conflicts; the voting-method set; user confirmation for the clinical and animal demotion; the real-machine re-run; version and release.
- ***** 326 THE RECORD HALF OF THE VERIFICATION CHAIN IS DRIVEN, and its honest degradation is measured.
  A recorded proposition comes back as a file-backed card with a content fingerprint, an owner and an undecided status. The background compile queue reports its jobs and its parallel ceiling. Archiving formal code stores it with a sha256 and does not claim it passed, and when the host exposes no subprocess service the archive still happens while the reason is named in the receipt - neither a silent failure nor a false pass. Recording "we could not formalise this" is a first-class outcome on that side rather than an absence of one.
  The consequence is the sharpest finding of the round: a proof that was archived but did NOT pass does not unlock the debate. The gate holds because the passed flag is false, so the ordering is enforced by the same record that carries the evidence. It also means, plainly, that on a host with no Lean toolchain the debate is unreachable - which is a containment fact rather than a defect, because the framework needs a working Lean path too, and it turns out to have the same four knobs already: command, timeout, asynchronous switch and parallel ceiling.
  The tally is now five equal, twelve property and four divergent over a hundred and nine green assertions.
  Still open: driving a PASSING Lean run once a toolchain subprocess seam exists, which is the remaining prerequisite for the debate itself; the stage-level precondition; the remaining check items; the drafting conflicts; the voting-method set; user confirmation for the clinical and animal demotion; the real-machine re-run; version and release.
- ***** 328 THE VERIFICATION CHAIN IS IN THE GATE: a standalone A/B file, one host context, thirteen assertions.
  The seam contract written down this round: the preset reaches its toolchain through ctx.get(subprocess), the service must provide resolveExecutable(name) and spawn({argv, cwd, stdio, graceMs}), and spawn returns a handle with a done promise yielding exitCode and signal plus a terminate method.
  What the thirteen assertions pin, in the order the preset enforces it: the institute starts with the seam in place, the roster publishes the childIds that seat an acting member, a recorded proposition comes back as a card with a fingerprint, a proof is written AND queued rather than compiled inline, the seam is actually exercised, the job lands settled with ok true and exit code zero and the file is promoted into the verified tree, a proof that has NOT been registered cannot open the debate, registering with op=formal_proof writes the ledger entry locked, and only then does the debate unlock. Two seated members then cast opposite boolean verdicts and the academician closes the debate so the aggregation runs.
  The aggregation is the round prize, because it makes C3 a two-sided measurement at last: v5r answers outcome undecided with a reason naming the participation gate and the unanswered members, while vmu keeps a three-valued tally whose undecided branch must carry a reason - and both cite the same D3 rule.
  A correction belongs here too. Last round I recorded that a second host context in the same test file seemed to resolve against an earlier institute. That diagnosis was wrong: the real cause was my file seam joining an ALREADY ABSOLUTE institute root under the temp directory, which mangled every path and made every write step fail while entirely logical steps still passed. Absolute paths now pass through untouched and the file is thirteen for thirteen. The lesson is a development entry: a file seam must tell relative from absolute, and when a step fails, suspect the path before the mechanism.
  The gate is green at a hundred and thirty-one with a hundred and eight suites, the curated quick subset expectation moved from thirty-three to thirty-four on purpose because this file joined it, and the package file list carries the new test.
  Still open: the stage-level precondition; the remaining check items; the drafting conflicts; the voting-method set; user confirmation for the clinical and animal demotion; the real-machine re-run; version and release.
- ***** 329 TWO MORE OPEN CHECKS CLOSED BY MEASUREMENT: D5a in both halves, and the aggregation boundary as an observable shape.
  The D3 vocabulary is real rather than nominal: the aggregation payload names who answered, who stayed silent and who declared themselves unable, alongside the arithmetic - m, P, the counts of true and false, and a mean.
  That makes the aggregation boundary observable instead of merely stated: with one true verdict and one false one the mean IS computed as 0.5, and the OUTCOME still stays undecided because members stayed silent. Opinion convergence and decision therefore sit in two different fields of the same receipt, which is exactly what the clause demands and what reading the code could only suggest.
  D5a is measured in both halves. A non-participant who tries to reopen is refused with a named rule - reconsideration may only be filed by a PARTICIPANT of that vote - while a participant succeeds, and the payload states the eligibility that admitted it as any-participant, together with onlyUp true and the before/after thresholds, so reopening can never soften the bar. A member who did not vote is refused earlier and differently: the object has not settled, because reconsideration targets an effective conclusion.
  Two smaller refusals were captured too: minutes answer that there is no meeting in progress because minutes are filed with the meeting that held them, and a reconsideration without a stated reason is refused with the ruling number cited.
  The verification A/B file now holds twenty-one green assertions, and the checking item that remains is the extension-cap half of D4, which was only partially observed: the reconsideration payload carries before/after thresholds and onlyUp, but no explicit cap on how many extensions a single debate may take.
  Still open: the extension cap; the remaining check items; the drafting conflicts; the voting-method set; user confirmation for the clinical and animal demotion; the real-machine re-run; version and release.
- ***** 330 THREE MORE CHECK ITEMS RESOLVED, two of them by finding that the answer is a DECISION rather than a gap.
  D4 asks for extension caps and a preset exit. The answer, measured in the ruling volume: the window and frequency layer of reconsideration is DELIBERATELY not built, on the principle that the framework recommends and never drives, so reconsideration has no window and no timer. No cap is therefore a decision rather than a missing gate, and the reconsideration payload still carries the before and after thresholds with onlyUp true, so reopening can never soften the bar. Reconsideration is idempotent as well: the same target, the same round and the same reason return a deduped receipt instead of piling up.
  D8 asks that observers, guests and temporary workers may be quoted, may speak, and never vote. All three halves are enforced in code and now asserted: temporary members are excluded from the active set, a vote attempt is refused by name with the ruling cited and with the note that even an authorisation cannot transfer a vote, and the refusal RELAYS the opinion to the voters rather than dropping it - which is the speaking half of the ruling kept alive.
  D5a is corrected in place rather than annotated: the row had said the initiator qualification was not expressed anywhere, and it is - a non-participant is refused by name, a participant succeeds, and the payload states the eligibility that admitted it as any-participant.
  The verification A/B file now holds twenty-five green assertions.
  Still open: the remaining check items (the broadcast triple and the notification window); the drafting conflicts; the voting-method set; user confirmation for the clinical and animal demotion; the real-machine re-run; version and release.
- ***** 331 THE LAST PIECE OF G2: the voting-method set is compared, and D9 broadcast triple is measured.
  D9 first: the aggregation payload carries who closed the debate and by which rule, WHY, and the result - the three elements the voting volume demands, all present in one receipt.
  Then the method set. Four methods are implemented here and each matches an adopted row: relative majority, option-style approval, the two-round runoff with an explicit round cap and top-N, and ranked instant-runoff. The ranked case matches the adapt verdict word for word - one collection round, one tally, and an elimination tie deliberately left undecided rather than broken by an implicit sort order.
  Confidentiality matches its three halves as well: the secrecy mode is chosen in advance, the FACT that a ballot was secret is recorded, and reopening cannot lower the bar.
  The divergence is the retention rule in action. v5r DECLINES veto power and cumulative voting outright, judging them architecture conflicts; here they exist as tunable options - a veto mode, a quadratic credit cap, a quota seat count - available and off by default. Neither side needs to change: the option set is a superset of the adopted rows and the veto behaviour is a setting rather than a hard-wired rule.
  The framework side also carries more than the adopted rows ask for: proxy voting with a chain depth, a roll-call order, recusal declaration and publicity, and a freeze mode linked to the meeting.
  The verification A/B file now holds thirty-two green assertions. Still open: the notification-window and effective-time items; the drafting conflicts between the three v5r volumes; user confirmation for the clinical and animal demotion; the real-machine re-run; version and release.
- ***** 332 G2 IS COMPLETE: the notification window, the effective-time notion and the drafting conflicts are the last three items, and all three turn out to have homes.
  The notification window is a pair of tunable keys - when a call opens and when it closes. The effective-time notion is expressed as a publication VERSION CHAIN: preprint, accepted, version of record, erratum, each step timestamped in the history, with a jump or a rollback refused by name alongside the current version and the legal next one. And the conflicts between the three drafting volumes are declared BY THE VOLUMES THEMSELVES: the ruling volume carries a consistency review of the decision volume faults, with a coverage verdict per row, plus two named discrepancies whose direction is stated - one an addition the decision volume never wrote, the other a tightening that was confirmed. So this repository only has to cite that list rather than re-judge it, because the ruling volume already followed the rule of not overriding the final draft.
  With that, every item of the meeting-platform comparison has a located carrier or a measurement: the eleven rulings including D5a, the five key gaps, the twenty-five gap items, the three hard clauses, the voting-method set, the notification window and effective time, and the drafting conflicts. The standing caveat is unchanged and worth repeating: locating a carrier is not equivalence, so every equivalence claim still waits for an A/B assertion, and the items that HAVE been measured are marked as such in the volume.
  The verification A/B file now holds thirty-seven green assertions.
  What remains before release: user confirmation for the clinical and animal demotion; the real-machine re-run; and the version and release decision itself, which needs user approval. Nothing else in the containment plan is open.
- ***** 334 ANSWERED A DIRECT QUESTION WITH EVIDENCE: what v5r-prompts.js is, and whether the preset still composes prompts in code.
  It is NOT the preset prompt engine. It lives at vibe-math-vmu/packs/v5r/prompts/v5r-prompts.js, it is a GENERATED file whose header says so, and it is written by scripts/generate-pack-prompts.mjs from prompt-corpus-v5/prompt-corpus-v5.json. It carries sixty-seven prompts across thirty-one kinds and seventy-six thousand characters, and it records the corpus sha256 inside the module so that any later difference is a check failure rather than a silent divergence.
  It is consumed rather than orphaned: packs/v5r/prompts/v5r-core.js imports its source path, hash and counts; both file lists register it; the regeneration chain includes it with a check mode; and a test compares every entry verbatim against the corpus. The generator own comment states the reason plainly - the containment table had reached fifty-six of fifty-six tools and the remaining axis was the prompt TEXT, which cannot be closed inside the framework because the framework holds policy while that line instructions live as text. Embedding the text as a hash-locked citation is how the content axis is contained without becoming a second drifting copy.
  The original mechanism is untouched: prompt composition lives in vibe-math-v5r.js as charterFor(member), briefBlock(member, roundNo), replySpec(kind) and initialPrompt(member, initialTask, mode, roundNo), and that file header states the invariant that every builder takes the member it addresses rather than reading the last member touched. Every A/B in this volume drove that preset itself - apply(ctx), the real tool registry, the real state machine, the childIds its own scheduler issues, the real meetings, ballots, reconsiderations, verification and Lean queue.
  So the two axes must not be confused: fixed TEXT is containment evidence, while code-composed prompts are the runtime mechanism, and the observation that the former looks hard-coded does not mean the latter was replaced.
  Also this round: the round budget was raised to eight hundred at the user request, and the demotion volume for the clinical and animal domains was opened, registered in both file lists and pointed at from the registry.
- ***** 335 PUSHING ROW-BY-ROW EQUIVALENCE: the lost-update gate is measured on BOTH sides, and it is the strongest EQUAL in the comparison.
  Same field, same precondition, same refusal family, same remedy. v5r creates a task carrying revision one, accepts an update that states the revision it saw and advances the counter to two, and then refuses a stale write by name - V5_TASK_STALE_REVISION, telling the caller the real revision and to re-read first. The framework side does exactly the same thing: the record carries a revision, an accepted write advances it, and a stale write answers VMU_TASK_STALE_REVISION telling the caller to re-read and retry rather than merging.
  The notable part is that the framework code was independently arrived at - it was added in round 318 as a fix for the very gap this comparison had found - and the two sides ended up in the same code family with the same field name. The only difference is a design one: v5r makes the argument mandatory, while here it is a switch that defaults off so single-writer callers are not broken, which is the tunability this framework is for.
  The verification A/B file now holds forty-three green assertions. The next rows to convert from carrier-located to measured are the messaging family, the member family and the Lean queue, all of which the same fixture can drive.
  Still open: the remaining row-by-row measurements; user confirmation is still needed for the pre-release full gate and for the version and release decision.
- ***** 335b ROW-BY-ROW CONTINUED: the messaging family is driven, and its delivery rules turn out to differ by WHO is speaking.
  A member speaking to the group delivers one message per recipient, each with its own id, and the SENDER is not among them. A private message reaches exactly the member named. The office relay, by contrast, reaches everyone including the academician - a different delivery rule for a different kind of speaker rather than one rule bent to fit both. The framework side keeps the same separation of concerns, with speaking into the record and quoting out of it as two distinct surfaces.
  The non-polling wait is bounded and says so: a too-short window is refused with the acceptable range named, ten seconds to one hour. The Lean queue meanwhile reports zero jobs, zero running and a parallel ceiling of one, which is what an idle institute should look like.
  The verification A/B file now holds forty-eight green assertions.
- ***** 336 THE PROMPT CORPUS IS NOW ASSEMBLED BY THE FRAMEWORK PIPELINE, and a previous answer of mine is corrected while doing it.
  The correction: asked whether the prompts are composed the way the original composes them, I said the framework side had no prompt mechanism. Wrong. The framework has owned a prompt PIPELINE for many rounds - five hundred and two lines with reserved order ranges, section registration, four-dimensional bindings, overrides and rollback, a variable whitelist, counted truncation and a deterministic assembly exit - registered as a service, documented in its own volume whose title is why a pipeline rather than string concatenation, and already wired to the plugin config. What was missing was the BRIDGE from the corpus into that pipeline.
  Built it this round: every corpus entry becomes a section in the pack range, one order per entry, kebab-case names, text verbatim. The new test proves properties rather than the existence of an array - pure-function assembly, byte-identical across runs so a snapshot is meaningful, provenance naming every contributor, a further section appendable at assembly time so the text is composable rather than frozen, and an out-of-range declaration refused by name rather than quietly re-ordered.
  So the answer flips: the prompt text IS now composed and regulated by the framework, through the same settings, config and middleware path as everything else, with more machinery than the original string building had. The other half of the earlier answer still stands: the original preset runs its own per-member, per-round composition, so handing the whole institute turn orchestration to the framework remains undone.
- ***** 337 THE GOAL FIRST OBJECTIVE STARTS: the turn loop itself is now on the framework side, designed first and asserted after.
  The user made this the first objective: the framework must run a member TURN - assemble, deliver, parse, dispatch - instead of leaving the loop in the original preset. The design went into a new volume first, then the face, then the gate.
  Five acceptance points all hold. Assembly really comes from the injected prompt pipeline and is refused by name when there is none, so the loop never composes text itself. Delivery is an adapter seam that refuses rather than pretending a member answered. Parsing distinguishes three failures with three names, and an unknown field is refused WITH the field named and the known fields listed, because guessing what a model meant is exactly what must not happen. The plan is a pure function giving each catalogued field exactly one owning face, and dispatch reaches that face once; a field whose face is not wired is counted and reported rather than dropped, so a half-installed host is visible. Assembly inherits the pipeline determinism, so a turn can be snapshotted and compared byte for byte, which is the precondition for the per-round A/B the objective also demands.
  Two existing guards each caught one real thing in the process, which is what they are for: the membership audit pointed out that a new design volume must be listed in the package file list as well as the installer, and the docs audit pointed out that every code the code can throw must be registered in the interface volume section 8. Both are fixed and the gate is green at a hundred and thirty-three with a hundred and ten suites.
  One process lesson belongs here too: the records script for this round was REFUSED by the runner because of a missing comma, and the command went on to commit the code anyway, leaving the documentation owed. The fix is a rule - every step of the chain must check its own result and stop on a refusal.
  Still undone on this first objective, and named in the design volume rather than glossed: the delivery adapter is not yet connected to a real host, the face map is not yet defaulted to the real kernel faces, the scheduler is not yet merged in for multi-round progress, and the per-round A/B against the original preset has not started.
- ***** 338 THE TURN LOOP IS WIRED TO THE REAL FACES, AND IT LANDS STATE. The ownership table now names real operations, read off a real kernel at RUNTIME rather than guessed: say goes to the meeting face speak, progress to records put, record to library record, propose_verify and proposal and motion to the meeting motion, verdict to ballotbox cast, task_create and task_update to tasks create and transition, formal to formal check, and the lean fields to mathjobs submit.
  The proof that matters is the landing one: an assertion builds a real kernel, runs a parsed task_create reply through the loop, and checks that the kernel task ledger actually grew by one. The loop lands state, it does not merely report success.
  Two lessons came out of the wiring and are recorded. The service bag keys are BARE names while the registry uses the vmu prefix, so the face lookup accepts both. And a missing face or operation simply does not enter the map, which means the dispatcher reports the field as not executed rather than inventing a call - a half-installed host stays visible.
  The ownership table is configurable: a caller binding wins per field while the declared default stays intact, and the orchestrator status reports the table it is actually using.
  The turn suite now passes twenty-three assertions. Still undone on the first objective: the delivery adapter is not yet connected to a real host, the service is not yet registered as vmu turn inside the kernel, the scheduler is not merged in for multi-round progress, and the per-round A/B against the original preset has not started.
- ***** 339 THE TURN LOOP IS A SERVICE, AND ONE TURN REALLY RUNS. Registering it inside the kernel is the step that makes the objective concrete: a kernel built with only settings, a root and a host now exposes kernel.turn, and one run call assembles the prompt from the pipeline, delivers it through the host seam, parses the reply strictly and dispatches it into the real faces.
  The measurement is blunt: services went from a hundred and five to a hundred and six, and driving run with a member and a phase produced fields task_create, one execution, and a task ledger that grew by one, with no original preset anywhere in that path. That is acceptance point one of the objective.
  Refusal semantics were measured too: with no host delivery seam the service refuses by name instead of pretending a member answered, and the refusal is counted on the status surface so a host that cannot deliver stays visible rather than quietly unproductive.
  The development volume gained the discipline the previous round taught about writing a face and an operation joined by a dot in prose. And the discipline added LAST round paid for itself immediately: the records script for this round was refused because a template literal contained a backtick, and because the chain now checks every step the refusal STOPPED the commit instead of shipping code with its documentation missing.
  What remains on the first objective: a concrete host deliverer, since the contract and the refusal semantics live here while the actual LLM or subagent delivery is the host concern; formalising the next-round wakeup policy as settings or a pack; and the per-round A/B against the original preset, which is acceptance point two and has not started.
- ***** 340 THE SELF-PROBE IS TIGHTENED TO ITS OWN STATED INTENT, AND THAT UNBLOCKS THE WHOLE INTERVAL. The gate that stopped the commit was an assertion inside the service audit, and the cause was mine: the probe defined "bound" with three patterns, the third of which counted a BARE MENTION between braces or commas as a binding - and the new turn-loop wiring passes the faces in a map literal, so mathjobs appeared there and the probe concluded it was still bound, which means the probe could no longer see a genuinely unbound service.
  The fix tightens rather than bypasses: binding now means CONSTRUCTED BY A FACTORY, which is precisely the question this gate exists to ask, and a passing mention was never a binding. The mutation companion turned green with it, so the gate was corrected rather than weakened.
  With that, the interval lands: the turn loop is a kernel service (a hundred and five to a hundred and six), one run call really runs a turn - assemble from the pipeline, deliver through the host seam, parse strictly, dispatch into the real faces, and the task ledger grows by one - a twenty-seven assertion suite, and the development volume entry about writing a face and an operation joined by three dots.
  Four guards stopped this interval at various points and every one was right: the service map wanted an entry, it also wanted the mapped identifier to be the property the kernel actually exposes (which prevents registered-but-unreachable), the maturity table drifted because the discipline example itself contained the three-part form the generator reads as a settings key, and the self-probe was too loose to bite.
  Still undone on the first objective: a concrete host deliverer, the wakeup policy as configuration, and the per-round A/B against the original preset, which is acceptance point two.
- ***** 341 THE PER-MEMBER GAP IS CLOSED, using the pipeline own variable injection and nothing hard-coded. Round 340 measured it: the original writes a different charter for every member, while the framework assembled the same text for two different members because the corpus is member-agnostic. The pipeline already supports template variables with a whitelist - member, role, phase, time, pack, task, settings and counts - an unregistered variable is refused by name, and template syntax inside a VALUE is escaped so a value can never inject a placeholder.
  So the fix is ONE section rather than a text per member: a role block in the pack range whose text says which member, which role and which phase this turn faces, with the values coming from the assembly context the turn loop supplies. The loop now accepts a role with the member and passes it through.
  Measured: sixty-eight sections, the sixty-seven corpus entries plus the role block, and the same turn now assembles differently for two members - seventy-eight thousand and forty-nine characters against seventy-eight thousand and fifty-one - with each naming its own member and role. The old divergence assertion stays as the reason the section exists: the plain corpus alone is member-agnostic.
  The discipline worth keeping: this added no mechanism. It uses the pipeline as designed, and the new section is editable through settings, config or middleware like any other.
  Still undone on the first objective: the default pack-to-pipeline wiring, a concrete host deliverer, and the wakeup policy as configuration.
- ***** 342 THE DEFAULT WIRING IS A DECLARATION, NOT A SIDE EFFECT. The v5r pack now declares the prompt sections it brings, and a pure collector in the pack loader gathers them, so assembling a host from that pack needs no hand-passing: measured, the collector returns sixty-eight sections and a turn assembled from the declaration alone gives two different members different text, which is the per-member property the last two rounds established.
  The design question mattered more than the code. A section cannot be registered when a pack is APPLIED, because the pipeline registers sections at construction and has no unregister, while leaving no residue on unload is one of the pack loader own stated invariants - so an apply-time registration would have broken the loader contract to save a line. It is therefore a declaration collected before the kernel is built.
  Two refusals keep it honest: a malformed declaration is refused by name instead of skipped, and two packs claiming the same section name is a CONFLICT rather than a silent winner. The manifest declares its sections through a getter, since the list is defined below the manifest and an eager read hits the temporal dead zone - the trap this repo has now hit twice, handled this time where it belongs.
  Still undone on the first objective: the plugin assembly site must merge declared pack sections into the prompt sections it passes to the kernel, with its insertion point already located; a concrete host deliverer; and the wakeup policy as configuration.
- ***** 343 THE DEFAULT WIRING IS A DECLARATION, AND THE ASSEMBLED TURN IS PHASE-AWARE. Two things landed, one of them a correction.
  The correction first: the design volume said the plugin assembly site should merge declared pack sections into what it hands the kernel. Measuring it showed that is impossible and that the design was already right for a different reason. The plugin builds the kernel SYNCHRONOUSLY, while importing a pack module is asynchronous, so it cannot load a pack and read its sections before constructing the kernel. And registering them when a pack is APPLIED is refused by the loader own invariant, because the pipeline registers sections at construction and has no unregister, while a pack must leave no residue on unload. Declaration plus collection before construction is therefore not a workaround but the only self-consistent shape, and the volume now says so, with the one-line form the assembly uses.
  The second landing is phase sensitivity. The role section carries the phase as a template variable, so one path covers many phases instead of a text per phase: measured, founding, normal and verify each name their own phase, the three assembled texts differ, and the same phase assembles byte-identically so a comparison is reproducible. That is the mechanism that lets the two-sided comparison against the original move beyond the founding round.
  The turn suite now passes thirty-six assertions.
  Still undone on the first objective: the two-sided comparison beyond the founding phase, a concrete host deliverer, and the wakeup policy as configuration.
- ***** 344 THE HOST BRIDGE MAKES "THE FRAMEWORK RUNS A TURN ITSELF" TRUE ON A HOST SEAM. The loop had a delivery seam and refused without one, but a host still had to write its own adapter; this round provides the one-line bridge, deliverViaSubagents, which takes the subagent seam and returns the delivery adapter the loop wants.
  Its contract is deliberately narrow, and each clause is measured. It sends the ASSEMBLED text and never composes anything itself. It labels the turn so a run can be traced. A host with no seam is refused by name. And a host that ACCEPTS the turn but hands back no reply is also a named failure, never parsed into "the model said nothing" - an empty answer and an absent answer are different events and the loop keeps them apart.
  The measurement is a whole turn through the seam: assembly from the pipeline at over a thousand characters, delivery through the bridge carrying the turn label, strict parsing, dispatch into the real faces, and a task ledger that really grew. No original preset is involved anywhere in that path, which is the objective's own sentence - that the framework run a member turn itself.
  The turn suite passes forty-one assertions.
  Still undone on the first objective: extending the two-sided comparison against the original past the founding phase, and formalising the next-round wakeup policy as settings or a pack.
- ***** 345 THE WAKE PATH IS COMPARED, AND THE COMPARISON PROVES THE OBJECTIVE OWN PREMISE. Round 344 ended with the two-sided comparison still short of a second phase, so this round went looking for the tool that advances the institute - and found there is none. Fifty-seven tools, not one of them advances a round; advancement happens through a wake, vibe_v5_nudge with a target, a reason and a next step.
  Measured on both sides. Without a target the original refuses by name and says the active member is empty, so there is no anonymous turn. With a target it accepts the wake and reports who was nudged, carrying who, why and what next. And then the finding that matters: after an ACCEPTED wake, the original spawns NO turn at all - the spawned-span count is identical before and after. Its orchestration lives outside its own tools, in the host loop.
  That is not a criticism of the original; it is a division of labour, and it is precisely why this objective exists. Because the orchestration lived in the host, only that host could run the institute. The framework now exposes the loop itself - open, assemble, deliver, parse, apply - so a different host can run it, and the framework applies the same rule the original does: a turn with no member is refused rather than seated anonymously.
  The A/B harness now holds fifty-nine green assertions.
  Still undone on the first objective: capturing the original's SECOND phase needs the host loop, which a fixture does not run, so the deeper per-phase comparison waits on running the original under a real host; and the wakeup policy is not yet formalised as settings or a pack.
- ***** 346 THE BOUNDARY IS PINNED DOWN, AND THE WAKE SPEC IS MADE ISOMORPHIC. Last round ended by naming a way to reach the original's second phase: drain the continuable children. This round tried it, and the answer is no - draining three times produced zero new turns. The original does not queue a continuable child at all when a member is nudged; it records WHO should be woken and leaves WHEN and WITH WHICH PROMPT entirely to the host. So the second phase of the original cannot be captured from a fixture at all; it needs a real host running the original, and that is another measurement of the same division of labour this objective exists to change.
  The half that IS in our hands was closed instead, and it is the more useful half: the framework turn spec is now isomorphic to the original wake. open takes who, why and what next; the turn record carries all three; and the delivery spec handed to the host carries to, why and next_step, so a host can supervise the turn it just started without reading the prompt text. The measured property is exact: the spec key set is the original wake parameters plus one trace label, which means the two sides wake a member with the same information.
  The turn suite passes forty-four assertions.
  Still undone on the first objective: capturing the original's second phase needs a real host running it, and the wakeup policy is not yet formalised as settings or a pack.
- ***** 347 THE FRAMEWORK NOW RUNS ITS OWN ROUNDS, AND THE WAKEUP POLICY IS A SETTING. Last round established that the original records only WHO should be woken and leaves WHEN and WITH WHICH PROMPT to its host - which is why only that host could run an institute. This round moves the other half in: nextTurn chooses the next member by a DECLARED policy, and drive runs several turns end to end, each one assembling from the pipeline, delivering through the seam, parsing strictly and dispatching into the real faces.
  The policy is a settings key, registered with its domain and read by the kernel, so a profile changes who gets woken without touching code: round-robin cycles the roster positionally, so every member gets a turn before anyone repeats, while least-recent prefers whoever has been woken least. Measured honestly, the two agree until the counts diverge - with everyone woken once they pick the same member - and the suite records that too, so the difference is demonstrated rather than asserted by name.
  Three invariants hold and are asserted. Nobody to wake is a LEGAL state that returns null, never an invented member and never a failure. The round bound is explicit, because there is no unlimited default. And a failing round stops the run and reports its number with the receipts so far, so a half-finished run is never reported as complete.
  The turn suite passes fifty assertions. Still undone on the first objective: wiring the round trigger to the scheduler face end to end, since each round can run but WHEN it fires is still a host tick, and capturing the original second phase, which needs a real host running the original.
- ***** 348 THE TICK SEAM: THREE QUESTIONS, THREE OWNERS. The last round left one item open - wiring the round trigger to the scheduler end to end. Measuring the scheduler showed that wiring it INTO the loop would be the wrong move: the clock is a seam by design, with vmu.schedule.timeSource defaulting to tick-only and the scheduler timer an optional injected seam, so WHEN belongs to the host and the loop should not take it away.
  What the loop needed instead was the tick SHAPE, and that is what this round adds: step runs EXACTLY ONE round, with WHO chosen by the declared policy and WHAT PROMPT assembled by the pipeline. The three questions now have three owners, and each is measured: when is the host tick, who is vmu.turn.wakeupPolicy, and what prompt is the pipeline plus the sections the pack declares - none of them left for a host to compose.
  The sharpest semantic in this slice is the split between two kinds of failure. A FAILED ROUND is an operational outcome, so step RETURNS it with its code and a null receipt; a MALFORMED ARGUMENT is a programming error, so it still throws, as drive does for a non-positive bound. The suite holds both: three host ticks produced three real rounds each landing in the task ledger with the member chosen by policy, a bad phase came back as a returned refusal, and the loop counts who it woke so a multi-round run is auditable from its own status surface.
  The turn suite passes fifty-four assertions. Still undone on the first objective: capturing the original second phase, which needs a real host running the original, and the remaining profile-side choice of which trigger line to use, since vmu.schedule.triggerVia already exists.
- ***** 349-350 THE TURN TOOL REACHES A REAL SESSION, AND THE FIRST OBJECTIVE GETS A SETTLEMENT SHEET. Round 349 added the entry point the loop was missing: the service and the host bridge existed, but nothing in a real session could reach the loop, and being runnable by a test is not the same as being runnable by a host. vibe_vmu_turn now offers step, drive and status, gated like every other face so the zero-mechanism promise holds, and the plugin-level suite proves the tool reaches the LOOP the plugin assembled rather than a copy of it.
  Wiring it produced two lessons worth keeping. The middleware wrapper calls spec.run while direct callers and tests call spec.execute, so a face offering only one fails at call time; and the wrapper STRINGIFIES what run returns, so a run that answers with a string gets it encoded twice and the caller parses back the same string. The fix is one core with two thin wrappers: run returns the object, execute returns the JSON string. Both are now measured, and the docs audit also caught that a host-registered tool must be in the interface volume section 3.
  Round 350 is bookkeeping with teeth: the volume now carries a settlement sheet for the objective the user made first, going through its three acceptance points one by one. Having a framework-side implementation is satisfied, and with evidence: the face, the service, the host bridge and a registered, plugin-tested tool, plus sixty-one assertions in its own suite and fifty-nine in the A/B harness. The gate holds. The per-round comparison is done as far as a fixture allows - per member, per phase, isomorphic wake spec, same anonymous-turn rule on both sides - with exactly one item named as out of reach in this repo: the original SECOND phase prompt is built by its host, so capturing it needs a real host running the original.
  Still open: user approval for the full pre-release gate and for the version and release decision.
- ***** 351 THE LAST UNPROVEN CLAUSE OF THE OBJECTIVE, NOW PROVEN: the prompt text is CONTROLLABLE through settings and at runtime. The objective sentence reads that the text must be assembled by the pipeline AND be tunable through settings and config; assembly was proven rounds ago, while tunability had a mechanism and no evidence. It has evidence now.
  Two paths, both measured. Declaratively: a profile sets vmu.prompts.overrides and the very next assembly obeys it, with no code change and still assembled by the pipeline. Interactively: override(section, text, by) changes the text and hands back the previous one so the change can be undone, and rollback restores the assembly byte for byte - tuning a prompt is not a one-way door. And the control is PERMISSIONED rather than open: an identity outside whoMayOverride is refused by name as VMU_NOT_PERMITTED, because being able to rewrite what every member is told is exactly the kind of power that should be named.
  The distinction worth keeping is that control changes the TEXT while the pipeline keeps the RULES: after a tunable edit the text is still assembled, ordered, bound, overridable and snapshotted by the same machinery, so tunability did not become a route back to hand concatenation.
  The turn suite passes sixty-five assertions, and the settlement sheet in the containment volume now carries this as its own row.
- ***** 352 THE CONTROL-FLOW FAMILY, MEASURED ON BOTH SIDES, AND THE TWO PAUSES TURN OUT TO STOP DIFFERENT THINGS. This is the most useful kind of comparison result: not a missing carrier, but a real semantic difference that both sides intend.
  On the original side pause is permissioned: a member is refused by name as V5_NOT_OFFICE, and the refusal says the office is the PROVABLE session root, which is the actor-resolution rule measured in earlier rounds showing up where it matters. The office may pause, and the receipt states what it stops - no member is woken again. Then the measurement that matters: while paused, a task is still created and a message is still delivered, because that pause stops the SCHEDULER and not the doors.
  On this side pause gates the doors: a write while paused is refused by name as VMU_STATE, and it also stops the scheduler, because the scheduler face takes an isPaused seam. So the two axes are both covered here while the original covers one, which makes the difference a superset rather than a substitution - and the superset is deliberate, since the framework side documents pause as a real gate.
  The revival condition is recorded too: a deployment that wants the original semantics, pause the driving but keep the doors open, uses the scheduler seam instead of pause, so the same machinery carries either policy. Resuming reopens the doors here and reports respawned members there, so neither pause is a trap.
  One sharp edge found while measuring: the kernel pause and resume take a STRING reason, and an object is silently stringified to [object Object]. That is recorded for the next round with its suggested fix, refusing a non-string by name, which matches the repository rule that nothing is silent. The A/B harness holds sixty-five green assertions.
- ***** 353 THE USER CLARIFIED WHAT "FULLY IMPLEMENT" MEANS, AND THE MEASUREMENT SPLITS THE ANSWER INTO LAYERS. The clarification: it means not depending on the original vibemath-v5r FILES at all, running the whole mechanism, orchestration and detail from vmu plus the v5r pack, its settings and middleware.
  First layer, code dependence: ZERO. A whole-repository grep shows no file under vibe-math-vmu imports anything from vibe-math-v5r, so the framework at runtime does not need the original files. The A/B fixtures do import them, but a fixture is a MEASURING STICK and not a runtime dependency - comparing against the original and not depending on it are not in conflict.
  Second layer, mechanism and orchestration: already moved. The turn loop, the wakeup policy as a setting, the scheduling seam owned by the host, dispatch into the real kernel faces, the verification chain and the Lean queue are all on this side.
  Third layer, the prompt TEXT: mostly there, and this round corrected my own earlier claim. The corpus carries sixty-seven prompts across thirty-three kinds AND ten compact charters of seven hundred and seventy to seven hundred and ninety-nine characters, plus the field catalogue and the fence requirement. My earlier probe searched only one field and wrongly reported the charters as absent.
  Fourth layer, and the real remaining item: the COMPLETE persona. Measured, the original hands the academician a persona of six thousand three hundred and thirty-four characters while the pack compact charter is seven hundred and ninety-seven, so the difference - the role block, the institute state, the tool catalogue, the priorities and the contract - is composed by the original CODE at runtime.
  That is the one thing left for the user meaning, and it belongs to the pipeline rather than to a copy: split the persona into SECTIONS declared by the pack, filled at assembly through the template variables the pipeline already has, so the text stays assembled, overridable and snapshottable instead of becoming a hard-coded six-thousand-character blob. The plan is recorded in the turn volume, no new mechanism is needed, and what remains is the section split, the variable sources, and a comparison proving the assembled persona matches or exceeds the original without the original present.
- ***** 354 THE PERSONA IS A SECTIONED DOCUMENT, SO "MOVING IT" MEANS MOVING SECTIONS. The last round ended by naming the one remaining item: the complete persona the original hands each member. Measuring it settled the design question in one step - the six-thousand-three-hundred-character persona turns out to be a STRUCTURED document, a hundred and seventy-two lines with ten numbered sections: the roster, the universal rules, the artifact formats, the role duties, the voting thresholds, the per-round rhythm, hiring, the task board, discipline and stopping. So moving it is not copying a blob; it is declaring those sections in the pack and letting the assembly fill them, which is exactly what the pipeline is for.
  The section table is written into the turn volume with what each section needs: the role and member variables the pipeline already has for the opening, a role BINDING for the role duties, the phase variable for the rhythm, the settings variable for the thresholds, and static text for the rest. Two slots need a data source rather than text - the research objective and the roster snapshot - and the volume says they must come from the loop as variables, never written into a section, because a frozen roster is worse than no roster.
  The capture step is built and run. A dev script drives the original once, splits each persona at its numbered headings and writes a persona corpus next to the prompt corpus; a shipped generator will read that and write the pack module, so no shipped script imports the original, which is the same discipline the prompt corpus already follows. The run captured all four members and the split exposed a bug of mine worth recording: the academician appeared to be missing a whole section, and the cause was that its heading is INDENTED by two spaces while my pattern demanded the bracket at the start of the line. With that fixed every member yields eleven blocks, and the gate stays green.
  Next: the shipped generator that reads the persona corpus and writes the pack module, then the assertions that the assembled turn carries those sections, then the comparison proving the assembled persona matches or exceeds the original with the original absent.
- ***** 357 THE USER FOUND THE REAL HOLE, AND THREE MEASUREMENTS CONFIRM IT. The user asked how the prompts are assembled at runtime, why the pieces cannot be seen, what v5r-prompts.js is for, whether its content is hard-coded, and what deleting it would change. All three answers are measurements now, and the user was right to be suspicious.
  First: the assembly IS the pipeline, with sections, ordering, bindings, overrides, counted truncation and snapshots. But the runtime plugin builds its pipeline from ONE source - the sections written in the config - and it neither calls the pack collector nor reads the pack declared sections at all. The pack texts and persona sections are therefore consumed only by the tests and the A/B harness, which is exactly why the fragments cannot be seen in a real run. That is the default wiring item recorded as owed in rounds 342 and 343, now shown to be the first hole rather than a nicety.
  Second: the corpus is a SNAPSHOT, not a template. Measured, the module carries sixty-seven texts across thirty-one kinds with a recorded hash, and inside them are twenty-four occurrences of a concrete round number - the sixth, tenth, twelfth, eleventh, ninth and first round appear literally - while sixty-six of the sixty-seven texts name a concrete member such as the academician by name. So the file proves the content was captured and gives material to fragment, but it cannot produce a correct next round by itself, because its names and round numbers belong to the run that was captured.
  Third: deleting it would break the pack load, the generator, the manifest and the tests, so it is not dead weight - but it would NOT change the prompts of a real run today, because of the first finding. That sentence is uncomfortable precisely because it is true.
  Two wires must therefore be added, and both are named with their plan. One: turn the snapshot into fragments by converting the variable parts into placeholders - the member name into the member variable, the round and phase into variables, the roster into the roster variable and the objective into its setting - so the pack carries reusable pieces instead of one frozen round. Two: connect the pack to the runtime assembly, which needs the pack loaded BEFORE the kernel is constructed, since the plugin builds the kernel synchronously while loading a pack is asynchronous; either the load moves earlier or the profile declares the sections through the existing settings channel.
- ***** 359 THE USER DIRECTED A CHANGE OF METHOD, AND THE SOURCE CONFIRMS IT: build from the original assembly LOGIC and FRAGMENTS rather than post-processing captured output. Reading charterFor in the original settles the question - it is already fragment assembly, branching on the member kind and pushing lines while filling live data.
  What the function reads, and therefore what the port must supply as variables: the institute name; the problem statement with its explicit fallback, which is what the earlier "hard-coded" (not set) turned out to be; the member id, kind, term, hirer and direction; the LEADER ID, which may be empty and which decides whole sentences; the quorum; the roster line; the library root; plus four constants or small helpers for the library spec, the academician organisation block, the common organisation text and the verdict round cap.
  Which parts branch, read off the source rather than inferred: the opening has three forms for academician, temp and researcher, and the temp form adds a term line; the division of labour has two forms depending on whether a leader exists; the roster block has three forms crossed with the leader condition and ends with a disclaimer that the authoritative list and quorum come from the per-turn state block; the general rules are fixed except item seven and except the three hard rules about debates, which are the same rules the verification chain measured; the library block is fixed; the organisation block uses a dedicated academician constant or a shared text plus temp variants; the important note about dispatch not changing the truth rules has two forms; the voting block has a voter form and a temp form and carries the quorum, the debate round cap and an academician-only line saying its vote is worth no more than anyone else; the per-round rhythm, context and stopping blocks are fixed; hiring and the task board each branch on the kind and on the leader condition.
  How the branches land in the pipeline, using what already exists: the kind branches become role bindings, since role is one of the four binding dimensions the pipeline has; the leader-condition branches become a TEMPORARY override applied by the loop before assembly and rolled back after it, so one set of sections carries both sentences and the override mechanism is already proven reversible; the numeric parts become the quorum variable and settings; and the roster snapshot becomes the roster variable, filled every turn, exactly as the original's own disclaimer demands. Two rules are carried over verbatim: a charter must never name a leader who is not on staff, and a brief must describe the member it is addressed to, which the original learned the hard way.
  The stopgap templating from round 358 stays green while this faithful port is built, and the next rounds re-express the branches and fragments from the source.
### 0.4 结论

## 0.5 未接线键的【三档结构】（轮 287 建表；**轮 290 据实更正数字** ✓✓）

> **为什么需要它** ✓：**`docs/04` 里未接线的【理由列是生成的】，绝大多数写着同一句话"经审计无承载者"** ✗ ⇒ **那个数字【不含逐键区分**，无法当作工作清单**）** ✓ ⇒ **改用【族 ＋ 是否有面提及该前缀】重建分类** ✓（**判据可复算：扫 `kernel/*.js` ✓**）。
>
> ★★★ **第①档的实测人口（轮 294；轮 295 追加 2 条）** ✓✓：
> ★ **轮 295 追加** ✓：**`vmu.math.formal.coqCommand`／`coqArgs`** ✓ —— **其族前缀 `vmu.math.formal` 在 `mathtools.js` 的 `UNWIRED_REASONS` 里【按名】登记为"需要 Lean/Coq 适配器接缝；除 sorryPolicy/coqTimeoutMs/axiomAudit/requireAll 外的助手细节未接"** ✓，**且 09 卷 L60 明写 Coq/Rocq "⛔ 未实现（仅设计）"** ✓ ⇒ **依判据属第①档 ✓（故不接 ✓）**。**人口 11 → 13** ✓。
>**扫遍内核的 `UNWIRED_REASONS`，共 45 个键带【面级受控理由】** ✓；**而在【当前未接线的 99 条】里，只有 2 条是【按名】登记的** ✓（**`vmu.math.network`：外部知识库检索未接（见 16 卷 N11 适配层）✓；`vmu.math.proof`：证明脚本生成未接（需要形式化后端）✓**），**另 5 条被更粗的前缀覆盖** ✓ ⇒ ★★ **故第①档由 9 条更正为 11 条** ✓；**其余 45 条受控理由多属【已接线】的族** ✓（**那些表是为未来声明保留的 ✓**）。★ **判据因此可复算** ✓：**"在某面的 `UNWIRED_REASONS` 里按名登记"** ✓。
>**未接线总数＝104** ✓（**轮 287 建表时 107 ✓；轮 288 接掉 1 ✓；轮 289 退役 2 ✓**）；**本表三档＝9 ／ 35 ／ 69** ⇒ ★★ **三者相加是 113，【大于总数】—— 这是【设计如此**：三档是【正交判据】（"这条能不能接" vs "有没有面提及"），**同一个键可以既是"有面提及"又"依声明不能接"** ✓，**故本表【不用于求和】，它只回答"下一步该看哪一档"** ✓**）** ✓。**族数**：① 2 ／ ② 20 ／ ③ 23 ✓（**判据不同，同样不求和** ✓）。
>
> ★★ **退役键的归宿（轮 289 实测）** ✓：**一个键退役后，它的【名字】必须离开所有卷，只留在本台账** ✓ —— **因为 `audit-vmu-docs` 要求【卷里出现的每个键名都必须在设置表里存在】** ✗ ⇒ **"就地标注已退役"不够，必须【连名字一起去掉】** ✓（**本台账是该审计唯一豁免的文件 ✓**）。

| R-1 | **范围蔓延**：vmu 想做"全维度框架"，容易变成"什么都做一半" | 永远无法定稿 | 每个能力面**必须先有验收判据**（11 号），无判据不实现；P0–P5 严格分期 |
| R-2 | **v5r 复用成本被低估**：v5r 是**单闭包单体**（11.5k 行全在一个 `apply(ctx)` 内）✗ | "复用"实为"抽出重构" | 复用判决以"抽出重构/重写"为默认；只对**已抽出的纯件**（数学模块/纯算法/提示词 builder）原样复用 |
| R-3 | **可观测面不足**导致"改了没反应"类问题 | 使用者失去信任 | R11：拒绝必须具名；设置必须显示来源层与热改等级 |
| R-4 | **中间件生态失控**（互相覆盖、难排障） | 行为不可预测 | 装载期静态校验＋干跑；冲突报错不静默覆盖；traceId 全链路 |
| R-5 | **工具链缺陷影响我们自身** ✗：本 agent 的 `read` 工具读 `app.asar` 崩（BigInt），且 asar 偏移应为 `8+headerSize` | 影响"读官方规范/包源码"这类工作 | 登记为**DSH 上游缺陷方向**；需要时走自写 asar 解析＋SHA256 校验（C′ 已验证可行） |
| R-6 | **长跑门禁偶发**：本仓已实测一次族套件在负载下的时序偶发（隔离跑 7/7 绿、全量重跑绿） | 误判为缺陷，浪费轮次 | 报告必须写"隔离跑/全量跑"；对时序敏感场景做**负载无关**改造 |
| **R-6b** | **v5r 变异族的正向对照偶发失败**（2026-10-09 实证 ✗✓；**同日已定位并双侧加固 ✓**） | 曾污染全量结论、并让每次全量多付 ~25 min | **根因已定位 ✓**：该族对 pristine v5 跑 `V5_SCENARIO=…`，且每个变异都是**子进程实跑** ✓；**负载下子进程会比断言"早"或"慢"** ✗（与 `tests/run-tests.mjs:211` 记录的同一课：run 是**活的** ✓）。**加固已完成（双侧 ✓✓）**：**正控**失败 ⇒ 一次**新工作区**重试 ✓；**变异族**未能按名变红 ⇒ 一次重试 ✓；**两者都被记录**（汇总行打印 `N retried, M green/matched on retry` ✓），且**重试仍红照样计入红** ✓。**证据** ✓：① `MUTANTS_POSITIVES_ONLY=1` ⇒ **107/107 正控绿**（**5 条首跑红、5 条重试即绿** ✓）；② `POSITIVES_SELFTEST=1` ⇒ `GUARD CAN FAIL AND IS COUNTED` ✓；③ `MUTANTS_SELFTEST=1` ⇒ **`RETRY CANNOT MASK A RED FAMILY`** ✓；④ 第 3 次 T3 里报红的三族**定向重跑全绿**（`S21 6/6`／`G2 1/1`／`S8 7/7` ✓，且写错名字时**具名中止 exit=2** ✓）。**残余** ✗：~~族仍要 ~25–33 min~~ ⇒ **已拆分 ✓✓（2026-10-10，用户批准）**：`tests/run-tests.mjs` 的 `VARIANTS` 把它变成 **3 个分片**（`shard=0/3` ＋ `replaceBare` ⇒ 裸作业即分片 0 ✓，另加 `1/3`、`2/3` ✓）⇒ **作业总数 132→134** ✓、**每片约 ⅓ 时长** ✓、**每片的判定行都打印 `shard=i/N`** ✓。**覆盖性证明** ✓✓：三片正控 **36＋36＋35 ＝ 107**（与拆分前**完全一致** ✓ ⇒ 无丢失、无重复 ✓），三片各自 exit 0 ✓；`update-doc-counts --write` 已重算（`total 134 / suites 68 / probes 66` ✓，4 个文件 ✓，`--check` 干净 ✓）。**S5 定点修复完成 ✓✓（2026-10-10）**：该族原 `expect`（`会议不变`）**不可达** ✗ ⇒ 回退变异本体、把 `expect` 换成它**真实且稳定**产出的具名红 ✓ ⇒ 定向 **7/7 ×3** ✓＋**曾经失败的分片 `1/3`：`56/56` 族、`36/36` 正控、零重试（8.8 min）** ✓✓。**负载下仍可能偶发** ✓（已被看见并被记录 ✓，且现在**有具名失败清单** ✓）。**第 4 例（2026-10-09 ✓）**：族在负载下**抛异常退出** ⇒ 汇总行全缺、**重试够不着** ✗ ⇒ 已加 `safeRunFamily`（**崩溃＝该族失败并打印 `FAMILY CRASH`** ✓，于是汇总必打印、重试必生效 ✓），并把该族的**估时按四次实测**由 906.9 s 校正为 **1800 s** ✓。 |
| R-7 | **环境判定错误**：用"本机没有 X"当测试前提 | 换机器/换盘符即红 | 11-§4.1：能力探测必须可注入接缝，两条路径都可确定性复现 |

| **P4 扩展面** | M3 脚本/工作流桥 ＋ M4 公开服务版本化 ＋ 文档自动生成 | P3 绿 | 脚本形态示例可跑；公开服务带 `apiVersion`；参数文档由 schema 生成且门禁一致 |
| **P5 硬化** | 静态门/变异族/语料/真机验收/发布门禁 ＋（可选）GUI 面板 | P4 绿 | `GATE_RELEASE=1` 全绿；11-§2 映射表无空缺；发布清单通过；（O2 若做则含 Client 门禁） |

## 2. 未决项按**主题**重组（替代"堆清单"）

> **读法**：每条给「现状 ✓✗／阻塞点／可选方案（≥2，含取舍）／建议方案／前置依赖／影响面／优先级／验收标准」；标 **✋需用户裁决** 的是必须由用户拍板的少数条目。
> **成熟度图例**：✓＝已验证；✗＝未实现；⚠️＝部分／有残留风险。

### T1 配置与装配面（`config.packs`／`config.modules`／`config.scripts`）

- **现状** ✓：三类装配面**已实现**；`status` 的 `settings.resolved` 逐键给出**值／来源层／热类别** ✓。
- **阻塞点** ⚠️：键路径演进中，文档若写死"某一层键路径"即失效。
- **方案 A（推荐）**：**通配家族文档法**——正文只用 `vmu.<ns>.*`，权威逐键留在设置手册卷；**取舍**＝用户需多点一次；换来"文档不随键名演进腐烂"。
- **方案 B**：在正文内嵌**完整逐键清单**；**取舍**＝复制即可用，但**每次键变更都要改多处文档**，且极易与实现漂移（本会话已见此类漂移）。
- **建议**：A ＋ 每卷末"未核项"兜底。
- **前置依赖**：设置手册卷（04）的逐键表维护流程。
- **影响面**：12（手册）、04（设置）、门禁中的文档计数审计。
- **优先级**：**P0**（用户照抄的入口）。
- **验收标准**：文档内**无**具体三层键名（红线①脚本可判定）＋ 家族表与 04 的家族集合**逐项一致**。

### T2 M3 脚本真机执行面

- **现状** ✓（真机已跑通仅在本会话最后一次）＋ **宿主缺陷已定位** ✗：不给 cwd ⇒ 宿主 `validateNoNullByte` 抛 `TypeError`。
- **阻塞点** ✗：宿主不接受 `cwd: undefined`；我方只能**规避**（永远传真字符串 cwd）。
- **方案 A（现行规避）**：接缝内 cwd 兜底链（调用方 → 配置 → 进程 cwd）；**取舍**＝脚本会被"意外地"在有 cwd 的环境里跑，语义略宽。
- **方案 B**：**拒绝**无 cwd 的调用（具名拒）；**取舍**＝更严格、更可预测，但把宿主缺陷**显性化**为用户错误，需要文档配合。
- **方案 C**：向宿主提缺陷单，等上游修；**取舍**＝最干净，但**不可控时延**。
- **建议**：A（已落地）＋ 文档写明"这是对宿主缺陷的调用侧规避" ＋ 保留 C 作为长期路径。
- **前置依赖**：宿主 side 修复（C）或维持 A。
- **影响面**：12 §16／§19；`host-spawn.js`（已改，属队友单）。
- **优先级**：**P0**（M3 是"真机可用"的门槛）。
- **验收标准**：真机场景 `vibe_vmu_script {action:'run',…}` ⇒ `{"ok":true,"ran":true,"exit":0}`（已达成一次）＋ 该场景进**非增量**门禁。

### T3 数学计算面（真机真实计算）

- **现状** ⚠️：接缝与键**已接入**；**真机真实计算未跑通** ✗。
- **阻塞点**：依赖 T2 的 spawn 通路稳定（数学面走同一接缝）。
- **方案 A**：**分两步验收**——先"探针可跑"（`op:'probe'`），再"最小计算可跑"（`op:'run'`）；**取舍**＝两次装置成本，但能把"通路"与"计算"两类失败分开。
- **方案 B**：一次性端到端；**取舍**＝省一次装置，失败时**无法区分**是我方形状、宿主管道还是算法本身。
- **建议**：A。
- **前置依赖**：T2 绿。
- **影响面**：12 §13-R10；14 §1 的"数学计算面"行。
- **优先级**：**P1**。
- **验收标准**：真机回执出现**具体计算结果数值**＋`exit:0`。

### T4 文档与实现的"成熟度标注"纪律

- **现状** ⚠️：多卷仍混用"目标形态"与"已实现"，且部分工具名出现在无标记行（本会话实测到 1 行既存违规）。
- **阻塞点**：**没有机器可判定的标注规则**。
- **方案 A（推荐）**：**标记词白名单**（`未实现/未接线/规划/提案/待实现/目标形态/尚未/roadmap/⛔`）＋ 文档审计**逐行**判定"未实现工具名是否与标记词同行"；**取舍**＝写作时略啰嗦，换来**可门禁化**。
- **方案 B**：纯人工校对；**取舍**＝零规则成本，但**必然回潮**（本会话已复现）。
- **建议**：A，并把它写进文档审计。
- **前置依赖**：审计脚本（归门禁负责人）。
- **影响面**：全部 docs。
- **优先级**：**P1**。
- **验收标准**：审计在**全部 docs** 上 0 违规（含既存行）。

### T5 安全面：脚本执行的权限边界 ✋需用户裁决

- **现状** ⚠️：脚本只允许**已声明 id**、启动器应为绝对路径；**没有强制沙箱**。
- **阻塞点**：**是否允许脚本访问工作区外路径**属产品取舍。
- **方案 A**：**硬限制**在工作区内（越界即具名拒）；**取舍**＝安全强，但会挡住"用户本来就想跑系统工具"的正当用法。
- **方案 B**：**默认宽松 + 显式开关**（越界需配置开启）；**取舍**＝灵活，但**误配置即风险**。
- **建议**：A（默认）＋ B（显式开关），并在 12 §17 写明。
- **前置依赖**：✋用户裁决默认值。
- **影响面**：12 §17；安全相关门禁。
- **优先级**：**P1**。
- **验收标准**：越界调用 ⇒ **具名拒**（有一条断言）。

### T6 发布与版本决定 ✋需用户裁决

- **现状** ✓：机器面全绿（非增量）；**发布本身未做** ✗。
- **阻塞点**：版本语义与发布时机**属用户决定**。
- **方案 A**：维持"**本地安装、不发布**"；**取舍**＝零外部影响，但使用者需自行装配。
- **方案 B**：正式发布（打标签＋registry）；**取舍**＝可分发，但**不可逆**（版本一旦推出即被引用）。
- **建议**：先 A，待 T2/T3 真机实证齐全后再议 B。
- **前置依赖**：T2、T3 验收标准达成。
- **影响面**：03／11（发布门禁）／全部用户。
- **优先级**：**P0（决策）／P2（动作）**。
- **验收标准**：用户书面决定 ＋ 决定后门禁行复跑绿。

### T7 跨工作区与多实例检索

- **现状** ⚠️：多实例**已实现**；**跨工作区检索未实现** ✗。
- **阻塞点**：索引格式与隐私边界未定。
- **方案 A**：**每工作区独立索引 + 显式"联合查询"开关**；**取舍**＝边界清晰，但用户需显式开。
- **方案 B**：**全局索引**；**取舍**＝查询方便，但**跨项目信息泄漏**风险。
- **建议**：A。
- **前置依赖**：存储层 schema 定稿。
- **影响面**：12 §18；07（耐久层）。
- **优先级**：**P2**。
- **验收标准**：联合查询在**开启**时返回两工作区结果、**关闭**时只返回本区（两条断言）。

### T8 观测性与"我声明的脚本到底跑了什么"

- **现状** ⚠️：`list` 只给 `{id, file, timeoutMs}` ⇒ 看不出**实际 argv／启动器**。
- **阻塞点**：观测面字段未设计。
- **方案 A**：`list` **增补**启动器解析结果与实际 argv 摘要（**计数式截断**）；**取舍**＝信息更多，需注意**泄漏敏感参数**。
- **方案 B**：只在**失败回执**里带形状（现状做法）；**取舍**＝零泄漏，但**成功路径不可观测**。
- **建议**：A（脱敏摘要）。
- **前置依赖**：形状摘要工具（已存在）。
- **影响面**：12 §12.3／§16；工具面 schema。
- **优先级**：**P2**。
- **验收标准**：`list` 回执含 argv 摘要且**敏感值被脱敏**（一条断言）。

### 主题—优先级总表

| 主题 | 现状 | 优先级 | 是否需用户裁决 |
|---|---|---|---|
| T1 配置与装配面 | ✓ | P0 | ✗ |
| T2 M3 真机执行 | ✓／✗（宿主缺陷规避中） | P0 | ✗ |
| T3 数学计算面 | ⚠️ | P1 | ✗ |
| T4 成熟度标注纪律 | ⚠️ | P1 | ✗ |
| T5 脚本权限边界 | ⚠️ | P1 | **✋** |
| T6 发布与版本 | ✓／✗ | P0（决策） | **✋** |
| T7 跨工作区检索 | ✗ | P2 | ✗ |
| T8 观测性 | ⚠️ | P2 | ✗ |

> **未核项** ✗：本节各条的"现状"依据**本会话实测**（真机回执、文档自检、栈）；**未**逐卷复核 00–15 的既有陈述 ⇒ 与旧卷冲突时**以旧卷的实测证据为准**，并回写本节。

## 3. 五处"最不合理／最不可实施"的**改法**与验收断言

> 来源：12 卷作者的本会话实测（真机回执＋文档自检）。此处给**工程改法**与**可门禁化的验收断言**，供联动 03/04/12/14。

### F1 「配置住在 `settings.yml`」与实现冲突 ✗
- **改法**：全部文档统一为"**配置来自插件行的 `config`**；运行时**无 YAML 文件读取**"；凡以 `settings.yml` 作配置源的表述**逐处改掉**。
- **验收断言**：文档审计检索 `settings.yml` ⇒ **0 命中**（或仅出现在"**不是**配置源"的否定句中）。

### F2 `declare` 动作不存在却出现在示例里 ✗
- **改法**：示例统一用**真实枚举** `list|run`；需要"声明"语义时走**配置面**（`config.scripts`），不要写成工具 action。
- **验收断言**：文档审计中 `vibe_vmu_script` 附近的 `declare` ⇒ **0 命中**；若命中，该行必须带标记词。

### F3 脚本必须给 cwd（宿主 `validateNoNullByte` 缺陷）✗
- **改法**：接缝**永远传真字符串 cwd**（调用方 → 配置 → 进程 cwd），并在注释与文档标注"**对公开宿主缺陷的调用侧规避**"。
- **验收断言**：真机场景 `vibe_vmu_script {action:'run',…}` ⇒ `{"ok":true,"ran":true,"exit":0}`（**已实测一次**）＋ 该场景进入**非增量**门禁。

### F4 `list` 只见 `file:"node"`（可观测性不足）✗
- **改法**：`list` 回执增补**脱敏后的 argv 摘要**（敏感值掩码、截断计数）。
- **验收断言**：`list` 回执含 `argv` 摘要字段，且有**断言**证明敏感值已被掩码（一正控＋一变异）。

### F5 「GUI 面板」未定却当改设置的入口 ✗
- **改法**：改设置入口统一为 **CLI／工具面**（`vibe_vmu_set`）＋**状态面回读**（`vibe_vmu_status` 的 `settings.resolved`）；GUI 仅作**规划项**出现。
- **验收断言**：文档中"改设置"的步骤**不依赖 GUI**；出现 GUI 的行**必须带标记词**。

### 五处改法的优先级与依赖
| 项 | 优先级 | 依赖 | 影响面 |
|---|---|---|---|
| F1 配置来源表述 | P0 | 无 | 全部 docs |
| F2 动作枚举示例 | P0 | 无 | 12、03 |
| F3 cwd 规避 | P0 | 宿主缺陷状态（T2／U6） | 12、`host-spawn.js` |
| F4 观测性 | P1 | 工具面 schema | 12、03、T8 |
| F5 设置入口 | P1 | 无 | 12、T1 |

## 4. 实现阶段的分批计划（"可调控性幻觉"的收口）

> **事实** ✓：设置表 **757 键** ＝ **51 已接线** ＋ **706 计划键** ✗（生成器原文：`757 keys (51 wired, 706 declared-but-not-wired)`）；登记面 **129**（23 有代码／106 提案 ✗）。
> **判据**：每批的**验收**必须能在**非增量**门禁上给出**具名红**；未过门禁一律不算完成 ✗。

### 批 1 · 会议与工作流（≈164 键 · P0）
- **范围**：会议/表决/工作流的未接线计划键。
- **依赖**：08 卷；`vibe_vmu_meeting`／`vibe_vmu_task`（已实现 ✓）。
- **验收断言**：① 每键一条**场景**（改键 ⇒ 行为按预期变化）；② 未接线键在 `vibe_vmu_set` 仍回执 `noConsumer: true`（**不得**误接）。
- **风险**：会议语义改动牵动 03 错误码面。｜**预计门禁**：非增量全量（含 08 族）。｜**用户裁决前置** ✗：无。

### 批 2 · 社会面与不变式（≈148 键 · P1）
- **范围**：17 卷社会面键（成员/雇佣/督导）接线。
- **依赖**：17 卷 S-1…S-6 定稿；`vibe_vmu_control`。
- **验收断言**：不变式被违反 ⇒ **具名红**（`VMU_INVALID_ARGUMENT` 族）。
- **风险**：影响面大，需渐进开关。｜**预计门禁**：非增量＋17 卷专题场景。｜**用户裁决前置** ✗：无。

### 批 3 · 归档与保留（≈124 键 · P1）
- **范围**：07 卷归档轨键（保留期、轮换、检索维度）。
- **依赖**：07 卷；审计落盘（已实现 ✓）。
- **验收断言**：审计**只追加**（写失败 ⇒ 操作失败）＋ 保留期到期行为可**断言**。
- **风险**：误删不可逆 ⇒ 先 dry-run。｜**预计门禁**：非增量＋07 族。｜**用户裁决前置** ✗：保留期默认值（政策）。

### 批 4 · 计算与形式化（≈171 键 · P0/P1）
- **范围**：09／15 卷其余 `vmu.math.*` 键＋统计模板＋Lean/LaTeX 面。
- **依赖**：真机 spawn 通路（cwd 规避已完成 ✓）。
- **验收断言**：真机场景出现**具体计算结果**（非 `NON-RESULT`）＋ `exit:0`。
- **风险**：外部工具链依赖重。｜**预计门禁**：非增量＋真机矩阵。｜**用户裁决前置** ✗：是否要求工具链随包。

### 批 5 · 合规与生命周期（≈82 键 · P0）
- **范围**：16 卷（生命周期）＋ 20 卷（安全/隐私/合规）的键与钩子。
- **依赖**：本文主题 T1–T10；`record.beforeWrite`／`script.beforeRun` 钩子面。
- **验收断言**：越权/路径逃逸/未去标识/无审批 ⇒ **具名拒**；kill switch 生效可**断言**。
- **风险**：合规默认值属组织政策。｜**预计门禁**：非增量＋安全断言组。｜**用户裁决前置** ✗：**kill switch 触发主体**、**许可/伦理默认值**（20 卷 §18）。

### 分批总表

| 批 | 主题 | 键量 | 优先级 | 依赖 | 用户裁决前置 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 会议与工作流 | ≈164 | P0 | 08 | ✗ |
| 2 | 社会面与不变式 | ≈148 | P1 | 17 | ✗ |
| 3 | 归档与保留 | ≈124 | P1 | 07 | 保留期默认 |
| 4 | 计算与形式化 | ≈171 | P0/P1 | 09/15 | 工具链随包 |
| 5 | 合规与生命周期 | ≈82 | P0 | 16/20 | **kill switch／许可／伦理默认** |

> **未核项（增补）**：本表键量为**按卷粗估**（依据各卷声明键数），**未**逐键核对归属 ⇒ 落地时以生成器输出为准；编号登记见 **14-§2**（主题 T1–T10）。
